Higher regularity and asymptotic behavior of 2D magnetic Prandtl model in the Prandtl-Hartmann regime
Jincheng Gao, Minling Li, Zheng-an Yao[10pt] School of Mathematics, Sun Yat-Sen University,510275, Guangzhou, P. R. China[5pt]

Abstract
In this paper, we investigate the higher regularity and asymptotic behavior for the 2-D magnetic Prandtl model in the Prandtl-Hartmann regime. Due to the degeneracy of horizontal velocity near boundary, the higher regularity of solution is a tricky problem. By constructing suitable approximated system and establishing closed energy estimate for a good quantity(called “quotient” in [ 18 ] ), our first result is to solve this higher regularity problem. Furthermore, we show the global well-posedness and global-in- x x asymptotic behavior when the initial data are small perturbation of the classical Hartmann layer in Sobolev space. By using the energy method to establish closed estimate for the quotient, we overcome the difficulty arising from the degeneracy of horizontal velocity near boundary. Due to the damping effect, we also point out that this global solution will converge to the equilibrium state(called Hartmann layer) with exponent decay rate. Keywords: Magnetic Prandtl equation, higher regularity, asymptotic behavior 2020 Mathematics Subject Classification: 76W05, 35Q30, 76D10, 35B40

1
Introduction

In this paper, we are concerned with the higher regularity and asymptotic
behavior for the mixed Prandtl-Hartmann boundary layer equations
that were derived in
[
10
]
from the classical incompressible
magnetohydrodynamic(MHD) system in two-dimensional domain with flat boundary.
Specifically, we are concerned with the following Prandtl-Hartmann equation
in
[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
:

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u\partial_{x}u+v\partial_{y}u-\partial_{yy}u=-\partial_{x}p_{E}(x,0)+\partial_{y}b,\\
&\partial_{y}u+\partial_{yy}b=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(1.1)

where
(u,v)
denotes the velocity field, and

b
is the corresponding tangential magnetic component.
The quantity
p_{E}(x,0)
above is considered prescribed,
and
\partial_{x}p_{E}(x,0)
evidently acts as a forcing term
to the Prandtl equations. In this paper, we are concerned with the
homogeneous Prandtl equations, that is
\partial_{x}p_{E}(x,0)=0
.

The Prandtl-Hartmann Eq.(
1.1
) are though of as evolution equation,
with
x
being a time-like variable, and
y
being space-like.
Thus, the Eq.(
1.1
) are supplemented with boundary conditions at

y=0,y=+\infty
, and initial data at
x=0
.
The quantity
L
appearing in (
1.1
) is though of as the time
over which we are considering the evolution.
For the Eq.(
1.1
), we will consider this system with the initial data

u(x,y)|_{x=0}=u_{0}(y),

(1.2)

and the Dirichlet boundary conditions

u|_{y=0}=v|_{y=0}=b|_{y=0}=0.

(1.3)

The far field is taken as a uniform constant state,
and hence, we also suppose

\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}u(x,y)=1,~~\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}b(x,y)=1,{\rm~uniformly~with~respect~to~}x.

(1.4)

Using the boundary conditions (
1.3
), (
1.4
) and
the second equation in (
1.1
), one can obtain the relationship

\partial_{y}b=-(u-1).

(1.5)

Substituting this equation (
1.5
) into the original Eq.(
1.1
), we have

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u\partial_{x}u+v\partial_{y}u-\partial_{yy}u+u-1=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u+\partial_{y}v=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(1.6)

with the boundary conditions

u|_{y=0}=v|_{y=0}=0,\ \underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}u(x,y)=1.

(1.7)

Since the vertical velocity
v(x,y)=-\int_{0}^{y}\partial_{x}u(x,y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}

creates a loss of
x-
derivative,
the classical method employed by Oleinik is to pass to the following
change of coordinates, known as the well-known von-Mises transform:

(x,\psi)=(x,\int_{0}^{y}u(x,y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}).

(1.8)

In terms of new variables, the original Eq.(
1.6
) translate into a quasilinear, degenerate diffusion equation as follows

(u^{2})_{x}-u(u^{2})_{\psi\psi}=2(1-u).

(1.9)

The corresponding boundary and initial conditions are

u|_{\psi=0}=0,\quad\underset{\psi\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}u(x,\psi)=1,\quad u(0,\psi)=u_{0}(\psi).

(1.10)

In this paper, the words “local” and “global” refer to the
x-
direction.
Similar to the classical Prandtl equation, higher regularity is a tricky problem
that arises from the degeneracy of velocity near the boundary.
Thus, our first target is to establish the local well-posedness
with higher regularity for the equations (
1.1
)-(
1.4
).

Theorem 1.1

(Higher Regularity Result)
.

Assume that
u_{0}(y)\in C^{\infty}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
satisfies the following conditions:

u_{0}(y)>0,\ y>0,\ u_{0}(0)=0,\ u_{0}(y)\rightarrow 1,\ y\rightarrow\infty,

(1.11)

and

u_{0}^{\prime}(0)>0,\ u_{0}^{\prime\prime}(0)+1=0,\ u_{0}^{\prime\prime\prime}(0)-u_{0}^{\prime}(0)=0.

(1.12)

Assume the initial data
|u_{0}(y)-1|
and
\partial_{y}^{m}u_{0}(y)
decay exponentially for all
m\geq 1
.
Assume also the generic compatibility conditions at the corner
(0,0)
up to order
2k-1
.
Then there exists a positive
0<L<<1
depending on the initial data
such that on
0\leq x\leq L
, the solution
(u,v,b)
of
Prandtl-Hartmann Eqs.(
1.1
)-(
1.4
)
obeys the following estimates for
0<2\alpha+\beta\leq 2k
and for
\gamma\leq k-1
that

\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta}u\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\gamma}v\langle y\rangle^{l-1}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta+1}b\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{0},

(1.13)

where the weight constant
l\geq 1

and
C_{0}
depends on
k,l
and
u_{0}
.

Next, our second target is to study asymptotic behavior
of solution as it tends to the equilibrium state.
More precisely, we study the global stability of the Hartmann layer
(\bar{u},\bar{v})=(1-e^{-y},0)
.
It is easy to check that
(\bar{u},\bar{v})
is a special solution of the system (
1.6
)-(
1.7
).
Since the higher regularity result in Theorem
1.1

require the compatibility condition on the initial data,
thus we only investigate the asymptotic behavior result of solution
in low regularity initial data.
Specifically, we have the asymptotic behavior result as follows:

Theorem 1.2

(Asympototic Behavior Result)
.

Assume that
u_{0}(y)>0
for
y>0
;
u_{0}(0)=0
,
u_{0}^{\prime}(0)>0
,
u_{0}(y)\rightarrow 1
as
y\rightarrow\infty
;
u_{0}(y)
,
u^{\prime}_{0}(y)
,
u^{\prime\prime}_{0}(y)
are bounded for
0\leq y<\infty
and satisfy the Hölder condition. Moreover, assume that for small
y
the following compatiblity condition is satisfied at the point
(0,0)
:

-u^{\prime\prime}_{0}(y)+u_{0}(y)-1=O(y^{2}).

For a given real number
\gamma_{0}>1
,
suppose that the initial data satisfy
f(0)\leq\gamma_{0}
.
Furthermore, there exists a positive constanst
\sigma_{0}
depending on
\gamma_{0}
such that

\|\frac{\phi_{0}}{u_{0}^{3/2}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{0\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{L\phi_{0}}{u_{0}^{3/2}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|(L\phi_{0})_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\leq\sigma_{0},

(1.14)

where
\phi(x,\psi):=u^{2}(x,y(x,\psi))-\bar{u}^{2}(y(\psi))
,
f(x):=\underset{x^{\prime}\in I}{\sup}\Big\{\|\frac{u}{\bar{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}},\|\frac{\bar{u}}{u}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\Big\}
, the operator
L\phi:=-u\phi_{\psi\psi}+2\frac{\phi}{\bar{u}(u+\bar{u})}

and
(\bar{u},\bar{v}):=(1-e^{-y},0)
be the Hartmann layer.
Then there exists a global strong solution
(u,v)
to the
Prandtl-Hartmann system (
1.6
).
Moreover, the solution satisfies the decay estimate:

\displaystyle\|u(x,y)-\bar{u}(y)\|_{H^{2}_{y}}+\|u_{y}(x,y)-\bar{u}_{y}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq Ce^{-x},

(1.15)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
x
.

Remark 1.1
.

Since we apply the von-Mises transformation to overcome the loss of
x
derivative,
the difference
\phi(x,\psi)
satisfies the new system

\phi_{x}+L\phi=0

(1.16)

in the new variable
(x,\psi)
.
Then, we establish the closed estimate for the system (
1.16
)
under the small condition (
1.14
).
This condition can be expressed in terms of the initial data
u_{0}(y)
;
however, we only give condition (
1.14
)
for the sake of simplicity.

Remark 1.2
.

Denote
\bar{b}_{y}:=(1-\bar{u})
, then we apply the relation (
1.5
)
and decay estimate (
1.15
) to obtain

\|b_{y}(x,y)-\bar{b}_{y}(y)\|_{H^{2}_{y}}+\|b_{yy}(x,y)-\bar{b}_{yy}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq Ce^{-x},

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
x
.

We now review some related works involving the classical Prandtl
and MHD Prandtl type equations to the problem studied in this paper.

(I)Some results for nonstationary boundary layer equations.

The vanishing viscosity limit of the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations that,
in a domain with Dirichlet boundary condition, is an important problem in
both physics and mathematics.
As the viscosity coefficient tends to zero, the solution undergoes a sharp transition
from a solution of the Euler system to the zero non-slip boundary condition on boundary of the
Navier-Stokes system. This sharp transition will lead to the formation of the boundary layer.
Indeed, Prandtl
[
43
]
derived the Prandtl equations for boundary layer
from the incompressible Navier-Stokes equations with non-slip boundary condition.
Now, let us introduce the related matter of well-posedness results for the Prandtl equation.
If the tangential velocity field in the normal direction to the boundary satisfies
the monotonicity condition, Oleinik
[
42
,
41
]
applied the Crocco transform
to establish the global-in-time regular solutions on
[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}

for small
L
, and local-in-time solutions on
[0,L]\times\mathbb{R}_{+}
for arbitrary
large by finite
L
.
Under the monotonicity condition and a favorable pressure gradient of the Euler flow, the
global-in-time weak solutions were obtained in
[
49
]
for arbitrarily
L
.
It should be noted that all of the above results are achieved by using Crocco transformations.
By taking care of the cancelation in the convection term to overcome the
loss of derivative in the tangential direction of velocity, the researchers
in
[
1
]
and
[
39
]
independently used the simply
energy method to establish well-posedness theory for the
two-dimensional Prandtl equations in the framework of Sobolev space.
For more results in this direction, the interested readers can refer to
the well-posedness results in the analytic or Geverey setting without monotonicity

[
44
,
45
,
29
,
31
,
38
,
22
,
8
]
,
ill-posedness results in the Sobolev setting without monotonicity

[
7
,
9
]
, generic invalidity of boundary
layer expansions in the Sobolev spaces
[
11
,
12
,
13
,
19
,
14
,
15
,
16
]

and references therein.

(II)Some results for stationary boundary layer equations.

For the classical stationary Prandtl equation, the local-in-time well-posedness result
was obtained by Oleinik
[
40
]
, who used the von-Mises transformation
and maximum principle. Due to the the degenerate property of horizontal velocity
near the boundary, it is hard to obtain the higher regularity for the stationary
Prandtl equation. This problem was settled by Guo and Iyer
[
18
]

since they found the good unknown quantity to establish the closed energy estimate.
For the incompressible steady Navier-Stokes equations, Guo and Nguyen
[
20
]
justified the boundary layer expansion for the flow with a non-slip boundary
condition on a moving plate.
This result has been extended to the case of a rotating disk and to the case
of nonshear Euler flows(
[
23
,
24
]
).
Recently, Guo and Iyer
[
17
]
studied the boundary layer expansion for the small viscous flows with the classical no slip boundary conditions or on the static plate.
This work was extend to the case of global theory in the
x-
variable
for a large class of boundary layer with sharp decay rates.
For more results about the boundary layer expansion, the reader should consult

[
30
,
5
,
26
,
25
,
27
]
.
In terms of the asymptotic behavior of the solution, Serrin
[
46
]

used the maximum principle techniques to single out that
similarity solution as those which asymptotically develop downstream, whatever may be the state of motion at the initial position at
x=0
.
In the case of localized data near the Blasius solution, Iyer
[
28
]

applied the energy method instead of maximum principle method for the good known quantity(called “quotient”) to establish specific convergence rate.

(III)Some results for Prandtl type equation influencing magnetic field.

Under the influence of electro-magnetic field, the system of
magnetohydrodynamics(denoted by MHD) is a fundamental system
to describe the movement of electrically conducting fluid,
for example plasmas and liquid metals(cf.
[
2
]
).
By asymptotic analysis of the incompressible MHD system, Gérard-Varet
and Prestipino
[
10
]
established a systematic derivation of boundary layer
models in MHD. The most important point is that they performed some stability analysis
for the boundary layer system, and emphasized the stabilizing effect of the magnetic field.
At the same time, Liu, Xie and Yang
[
35
]
studied the local-in-time
well-posedness for the Prandtl type equation in MHD that is built if both the hydrodynamic Reynolds numbers and magnetic Reynolds numbers tend to infinity at the same rate.
Since they found that magnetic field has good effect on stability, the well-posedness
result holds true under the condition that the initial tangential magnetic field is not zero
instead of the monotonicity condition on the tangential velocity field requiring in classical
Prandtl equation
[
41
]
.
This well-posednss result is generalized to the case of inhomogeneous incompressible flow
that is Hyperbolic-parabolic coupled equations in
[
6
]
.
Furthermore, the Prandtl ansatz boundary layer expansion for the unsteady MHD system
was justified
[
36
]
when no-slip boundary and perfect conducting boundary
conditions are imposed on velocity field and magnetic field respectively.
Recently there are many mathematical results on two dimensional MHD boundary layer system,
for the almost global existence
[
32
]
,
ill-posedness results
[
34
,
33
]
,
lifespan of solution with analytic perturbation of general shear flow
[
48
]
,
boundary layer expansions for steady MHD over a moving plate
[
4
]
,
and local-in-time well-posedness for compressible MHD boundary layer
[
21
]
.
For the 2D MHD boundary layer equations in the mixed Prandtl and
Hartmann regime derived by formal multiscale expansion in
[
10
]
,
Xie and Yang
[
47
]
obtained the global existence of solutions
with analytic regularity.
If the initial data is near the Hartmann layer, the solution of Prandtl-Hartmann system
is globally well-posedness in Sobolev space and will converge to the Hartmann
layer with exponent decay rate in
[
3
]
.
However, there is no result investigating the global well-posedness
and convergence rate for the steady Prandtl-Hartmann system.
The difficulty comes from the degeneracy of horizontal velocity near the boundary

y=0
.

Thus, motivated by
[
18
]
, our first target is to establish
the higher regularity result of solution for the Prandtl-Hartmann system (
1.6
).
The advantage of our method here is to constructed new approximated system such that
higher regularity result can be obtained without using the property of lower regular solution.
Motivated by the recent work
[
28
]
, our second target is to use the energy method to build the asymptotic behavior of global solution for the Prandtl-Hartmann system (
1.6
). Due to the damping effect, we point out that this global solution will converge
to the equilibrium state(called Hartmann profile) with exponent decay rate.

Notations:
Through this paper, all constant
C
may be different in different lines.
Subscript(s) of a constant illustrates the dependence of the constant, for example,

C_{s}
is a constant depending on
s
only.
We write
o_{L}(1)
to refer to a constant that is bounded by some unspecified, perhaps small,
power of
L
: that is,
a=o_{L}(1)
if
|a|\leq CL^{\theta}
for some
\theta>0
.
We also write the
C^{\infty}
cutoff function:

\chi(y):=\left\{\begin{aligned} &1,\quad y\in[0,1),\\
&0,\quad y\in(2,\infty),\end{aligned}\right.

(1.17)

and
\chi^{\prime}(y)\leq 0
for all
y>0
.

A\lesssim B

(A\gtrsim B)
represents that there exists a positive constant
C
such that
A\leq CB

(A\geq CB)
.
And
A\sim B
stands for
A\lesssim B
and
A\gtrsim B
.
Finally,
\mathcal{P}_{i}(\cdot,\cdot)
stands for
a polynomial function independent of
\epsilon
,
and the index
i
denote it changing from line to line.

2
Difficulties and outline of our approach

The main goal of this section is to explain main difficulties of proving
Theorems
1.1
and
1.2
as well as our strategies for overcoming them.
In order to establish the well-posedness in some higher regularity Sobolev space
and asymptotic behavior for the Prandtl type Eq.(
1.6
), the main difficulty
comes from the vertical velocity
v=-\partial_{y}^{-1}\partial_{x}u
creating a loss of
x-
derivative.
Thus, we will introduce the method to overcome this difficulty.

First of all, we state the main idea to establish higher regularity of
Prandtl type Eq.(
1.6
) in Theorem
1.1
.
In order to overcome the loss of
x-
derivative coming from the vertical velocity,
the classical method is to apply the so-called von-Mises transformation
that transforms the original Eq.(
1.6
) into a single
quasi-linear parabolic equation.
Due to the degenerate property of horizontal velocity on the boundary, it is not easy
to establish higher regularity for this quasi-linear parabolic equation.
In this respect, Guo and Iyer
[
18
]
settled this tricky subject,
arising from the classical steady Prandtl equation,
by establishing the energy estimate for the good unknown quantity
q:=\frac{v}{\overline{u}}

via the linear derivative Prandtl equation.
Here the quantity
\overline{u}
is the solution of classical steady Prandtl equation
obtained by Oleinik
[
42
]
.
In other words, this method depends on the property of lower regular solution of
original steady Prandtl equation.

Since there is no result about the well-posedness of Eq.(
1.6
), we hope to
find a method to establish the well-posedness in higher regularity space
without using the property of lower regular solution
.
Thus, in this paper, we consider the following approximated system

\left\{\begin{aligned} &u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}+v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}-\partial_{yy}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon=0,\\
&\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}+\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}=0,\end{aligned}\right.

(2.1)

with the boundary condition

u^{\epsilon}|_{y=0}=\epsilon,\quad\lim_{y\rightarrow+\infty}u^{\epsilon}=1+\epsilon,\quad v^{\epsilon}|_{y=0}=0,

(2.2)

and the initial data

u^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}=u_{0}^{\epsilon}(y):=u_{0}(y)+\epsilon,

(2.3)

for any parameter
\epsilon>0
.
Since the initial and boundary conditions of horizontal velocity are positive,
we can follow the idea as Guo and Nguyen(see Lemma 2.2 in
[
20
]
)
to obtain the local well-posedness in higher regularity space for any fixed
\epsilon>0
.
However, it is worth nothing that the life time interval
[0,L^{\epsilon}]
depends
on the parameter
\epsilon
.
As we hope that the solution
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
of approximate system (
2.1
)
with positive boundary condition will converge to the solution
(u,v)

of original Prandtl type Eq.(
1.6
)
as
\epsilon
tends to zero.
To achieve the target, we need to obtain the uniform a priori estimates of solution

(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
on existence time independent of
\epsilon
.
Thanks to the recent paper
[
18
]
, the so called quotient
\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})

is a good unknown for us to avoid the loss of derivative
and the degeneracy of quasi-linear parabolic equation to establish closed a priori estimate.
And hence, we need to control the quantity
\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}
by the good unknown
\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})

by using the Hardy inequality.
Thus, our method here is to establish the energy estimate including
energy part and dissipative one both with suitable weighted
\langle y\rangle^{l}
.
Then, the local well-posedness of the Eq.(
1.6
) in higher regular space
follows directly as the parameter
\epsilon
tends to zero.

Next, we state the main approach to obtain the asymptotic behavior of solution for the Prandtl Eq.(
1.6
) in Theorem
1.2
.
For classical Prandtl equation, in the case of localized data near the Blasius solution, Iyer
[
28
]
applied the energy method instead of maximum principle method for the
good known quantity(called “quotient”) to establish specific convergence rate for the quantity
\phi(x,\psi):=u^{2}(x,\psi)-\bar{u}^{2}(\psi)
.
However, he did not build any convergence rate for the quantity
u(x,y)-\bar{u}(y)
.

Since there is no result about the asymptotic behavior of Eq.(
1.6
), we hope to find a method to establish the convergence rate in physical variable
.
By using the method as in
[
28
]
, we first show the global existence of the solution for 2-D Prandtl-Hartmann system via standard continuity argument.
It should be noted that the term
\frac{\phi}{\bar{u}(u+\bar{u})}
in the equation (
1.16
) plays an important role as a damping term. Thus, we can get
that the difference
\phi
decays exponentially in
x
variable, that is

\displaystyle\|\phi\|_{H^{1}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{x}\|_{H^{1}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq Ce^{-x}.

(2.4)

More details of the proof for this decay estimate can be found in Proposition
4.2
in Section
4
.

The asymptotic behavior (
2.4
) occurs at equal values of the stream function
\psi
, but rather than at equal values of the physical variable
y
.
In order to obtain the decay estimate in
x
variable of the difference of
u
and
\bar{u}
as functions of
(x,y)
, as required for (
1.15
), we need further and more arguments.
For any given value of the stream function
\psi
, we denote
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
represent the corresponding physical variable for the flows
u
and
\bar{u}
, respectively.
Motivated by
[
46
]
, we decompose the normal gradient of the tangential velocity as the following two parts:

\displaystyle|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|\leq|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})|+|\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|.

The first part can be transformed into von-Mises variable and then controlled by the exponential decay estimate (
2.4
).
After some basic calculations, the second part about the solution
\bar{u}
of Hartmann boundary layer can also be controlled by the exponential decay estimate (
2.4
).
Therefore, we conclude that
(u_{y}-\bar{u}_{y})
decays exponentially in
x
variable in
L^{\infty}_{y}-
norm.
It then follows from the similar way above to show that
(u-\bar{u})
also decay exponentially in
x
variable in
H^{2}_{y}-
norm.
The main ideas and strategy of establishing the decay estimates in
x
variable of the difference of
u
and
\bar{u}
as functions of
(x,y)
will be given in Section
4.3
.

3
Local existence and uniqueness of Prandtl-Hartmann system

In this section, we will establish the local existence and uniqueness
of solution with higher regularity for the Prandtl-Hartmann system
(
1.1
)-(
1.4
) in Theorem
1.1
.
In section
3.1
, we will establish some uniform estimates with respect to the
parameter
\epsilon
for the approximated system (
2.1
).
The local existence and uniqueness of original Prandtl-Hartmann system
are be investigated in sections
3.2
and
3.3
respectively.

3.1.  Uniform estimate for approximated system

In this subsection, we will establish uniform estimate with respect to
the parameter
\epsilon
for the approximated system (
2.1
) such that there exists
a positive time
L
independent of
\epsilon
.
Motivated by the recent work
[
18
]
,
we used the divergence-free condition
\eqref{app-sym}_{2}

to rewrite the approximated system (
2.1
) as follows

-(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\right)-\partial_{yy}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon=0.

Apply the
\partial_{x}-
operator to the above equation
and use the divergence-free condition
\eqref{app-sym}_{2}
, we have

-\partial_{x}\left\{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{y}\left(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\right)\right\}+\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}=0.

Let us denote the notation(called “quotient” in
[
18
]
)

q^{\epsilon}:=\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}},

(3.1)

then it holds

-\partial_{x}\{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\}+\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}=0.

(3.2)

Due to the conditions of initial data
u_{0}(y)
in Theorem
1.1
,
there exists a small
0<\delta_{0}\ll 1
such that

\frac{1}{2}y\leq u_{0}(y)\leq\frac{3}{2}y,\quad\forall y\in[0,\delta_{0}]

and

u_{0}(y)\geq\frac{1}{2}\delta_{0},\quad\forall y\in[\delta_{0},+\infty).

Then, the initial data
u_{0}^{\epsilon}(y)
(see the definition in (
2.3
)) will satisfy

\frac{1}{2}(y+\epsilon)\leq u^{\epsilon}_{0}(y)\leq\frac{3}{2}(y+\epsilon),\quad\forall y\in[0,\delta_{0}]

(3.3)

and

u^{\epsilon}_{0}(y)\geq\frac{1}{2}(\delta_{0}+\epsilon),\quad\forall y\in[\delta_{0},+\infty).

(3.4)

Let us define the norms:

\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x):=\sum_{j=0}^{k}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\sum_{j=0}^{k}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

(3.5)

X^{k}_{l}(x):=\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}\sum_{i=1}^{3}\|\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{i}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

(3.6)

and

\displaystyle\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(x):=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{2}\|\partial_{y}^{j}u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,x])L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{\infty}([0,x])L^{2}_{y}}

(3.7)

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{3}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{j}v^{\epsilon}(x,y)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,x])L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}(x,y)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,x])L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(x,y)(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}([0,x])L^{2}_{y}}.

Thus, the parameter
\delta
appearing in cutoff function

\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})
(see (
1.17
))
will be chosen to satisfy
\delta\leq\delta_{0}

such that the assumption conditions (
3.3
)
and (
3.4
) hold on.

Proposition 3.1

(Uniform estimate)
.

Let
k\geq 2
be an integer,
l\geq 1
be a real number and
\epsilon\in(0,1]
,
and
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
be sufficiently smooth solution, defined on
[0,L^{\epsilon}]
,
to the non-degenerated approximated system (
2.1
)-(
2.3
).
The initial data
u_{0}^{\epsilon}(y)
is defined by (
2.3
) and
satisfies the conditions (
3.3
) and (
3.4
). Then, there exists a time

L_{a}=L_{a}(k,l,\delta_{0},u_{0})>0

independent of
\epsilon
such that the following a priori estimate holds true for all

x\in[0,L_{a}]
:

X_{l}^{k}(x)^{2}+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}\leq 2\overline{C}_{k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+C(u_{0})),

(3.8)

and

\frac{1}{4}y\leq u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\leq 2(y+\epsilon),\ (x,y)\in[0,L_{a}]\times[0,\delta_{0}];\quad u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\geq\frac{1}{4}\delta_{0},\ (x,y)\in[0,L_{a}]\times[\delta_{0},\infty).

(3.9)

Furthermore, it also holds

\sum_{0\leq 2\alpha+\beta\leq 2k+1}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq{C}_{k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+C(u_{0})),

(3.10)

where
C(u_{0})
is a constant depends on the initial data
u_{0}
.

Throughout this subsection, for some positive constants
k_{*}

and
k^{*}(>k_{*})
, we assume that the following a priori assumptions:

k_{*}(y+\epsilon)\leq u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\leq k^{*}(y+\epsilon),\quad(x,y)\in[0,L^{\epsilon}]\times[0,\delta_{0}],

(3.11)

and

u^{\epsilon}(x,y)\geq k_{*}(\delta_{0}+\epsilon),\quad(x,y)\in[0,L^{\epsilon}]\times[\delta_{0},+\infty)

(3.12)

hold on.
Here the parameter
\delta_{0}
is defined before.

Lemma 3.2
.

Let
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
be the smooth solution, defined on
[0,L^{\epsilon}]
,
of the approximated equations (
2.1
)-(
2.3
).
Under the conditions of (
3.11
) and (
3.12
), then we have the
following estimates:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k^{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2});

(3.13)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}v\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2});

(3.14)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4});

(3.15)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4});

(3.16)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{4}v\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k^{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4});

(3.17)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

(3.18)

where
l\geq 1
and
k\geq 2
.

Proof.

Step 1: Using the definition of
q^{\epsilon}
(see (
3.1
)), we can get

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}+2\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle+2\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{11}+I_{12}+I_{13}+I_{14}+I_{15}+I_{16}.

Using estimates (
A.4
)-(
A.6
)
in Appendix
A
, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|I_{11}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x);

\displaystyle\|I_{12}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x);

\displaystyle\|I_{13}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x);

\displaystyle\|I_{15}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x));

\displaystyle\|I_{16}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)).

Based on the combination of the above estimates, we have for all
l\geq 1

\displaystyle\|I_{11}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|I_{12}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|I_{13}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|I_{15}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|I_{16}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.19)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Let us write

I_{14}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi):=I_{141}+I_{142}.

First of all, let us deal with the
I_{141}
term.
Due to
k\geq 2
, using Hölder inequality,
estimates (
A.3
) and (
A.8
), we can obtain

\displaystyle\|I_{141}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\delta_{0}+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.20)

\displaystyle+C_{k}(\delta_{0}+\epsilon)^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k,k_{*},k^{*},\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{H^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+o_{L}(1)C_{k,k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Here the condition
k\geq 2
implies directly
[\frac{k-1}{2}]\leq k-2
.
Using the Hölder inequality, we have

\displaystyle\|I_{142}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|{\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}}(1-\chi)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

(3.21)

Away from the boundary
y=0
, we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

Substituting the estimate (
3.22
) into (
3.21
)
and applying the divergence-free condition, we have

\|I_{142}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|{\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

which, together with estimate (
3.20
), yields directly

\|I_{14}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

This and the estimate (
3.19
) imply directly for all
l\geq 1

\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k^{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}),

which yields the estimate (
3.13
).

Step 2: Obviously, it holds

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle=\partial_{x}^{k}(\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}q^{\epsilon})+\partial_{x}^{k}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon})

\displaystyle=(\partial_{y}u\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon})+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=I_{21}+I_{22}+I_{23}.

Using Hölder inequality and estimate (
A.6
),
it holds for all
l\geq 1

\displaystyle\|I_{21}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}+\sqrt{L}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l}(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x).

Using the divergence-free condition,
and estimates (
A.6
) and (
A.7
), it holds for
k\geq 2

\displaystyle\|I_{22}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k-1}_{l}(x)

\displaystyle+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1}_{l}(x))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Using the divergence-free condition,
and estimates (
A.4
) and (
A.5
), it holds for
k\geq 2

\displaystyle\|I_{23}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)

\displaystyle+o_{L}(1)C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1}_{l}(x))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Thus, we have for all integer
k\geq 2
and
l\geq 1

\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

This implies directly the estimate (
3.14
).

Step 3:
Using the equation (
3.2
), it holds

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.23)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{j-1}\|\partial_{x}^{j_{1}-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j-j_{1}-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{31}+I_{32}+I_{33}+I_{34}.

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle|I_{32}|\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.24)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)(1+\|u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}_{0}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

Using divergence-free condition, estimates (
A.4
) and (
3.14
),
it is easy to check that

\displaystyle|I_{33}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

(3.25)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon})\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

where we have used the estimate in the last inequality

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon})\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle[(1-\chi)+\chi]\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Here the above terms can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})(\|\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C(\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}),

and

\displaystyle\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle[(1-\chi)+\chi]\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.26)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\chi|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})+(1+\|\partial_{y}u_{0}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle(1-\chi)|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle(1-\chi)\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Similarly, we can get

\displaystyle|I_{34}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.27)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})^{2}\mathcal{E}^{k-1}_{l}(x).

Substituting estimates (
3.24
), (
3.25
) and (
3.27
)
into (
3.23
), then it holds for all
l\geq 1

\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

which, together with estimate (
3.14
), yields directly

\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

which yields the estimate (
3.15
).

Step 4:
Integrating by part, using Hardy inequality (
A.1
),
Hölder and Cauchy inequalities, it holds true

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dy

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}(\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v\langle y\rangle^{2l}+2l\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l-2})|dy

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+2l\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l-1}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq C_{l}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}.

Then, for any integer
k\geq 2
and
l\geq 1
,
we use the estimates (
3.14
) and (
3.15
) to obtain

\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

which yields the estimate (
3.16
).

Step 5:
Using the equation (
3.2
), we have for any integer
k\geq 2

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}=

\displaystyle\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}+(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}+2\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon})\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{x}^{j}\{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}:=I_{41}+I_{42}+I_{43}+I_{44}+I_{45}.

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|I_{42}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq C(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2})\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

\displaystyle\|I_{43}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq o_{L}(1)(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

To deal with the term
I_{44}
, we can follow the idea
as terms
I_{33}
.
Let us write

\displaystyle I_{44}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{j-1}C^{k}_{j}C^{j}_{j_{1}}\partial_{x}^{j_{1}}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j_{1}}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}:=I_{441}+I_{442}+I_{443}.

Using estimate (
3.26
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|I_{441}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

Similarly, using estimate (
A.4
), we can obtain for all
k\geq 2

\displaystyle\|I_{442}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle+C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\times\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},k}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

and

\displaystyle\|I_{443}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{k-2}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k-2}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{k-2}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{x}^{k-2}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},k}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

Collecting the estimates from terms
I_{441}
to
I_{443}
, we get that

\|I_{44}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

Finally, let us deal with the term
I_{45}
.
Indeed, we use the divergence-free condition to write

\displaystyle I_{45}=

\displaystyle\sum_{j=1}^{k}2C^{k}_{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}\sum_{j_{1}=1}^{j-1}2C^{k}_{j}C_{j_{1}}^{j}\partial_{x}^{j_{1}-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j-j_{1}-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{451}+I_{452}.

Using divergence-free condition, estimates (
3.26
) and (
A.4
), we can get

\displaystyle\|I_{451}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(1+\|u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l-1}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle+C_{k,l}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

and

\displaystyle\|I_{452}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}^{2}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2})\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},k,l}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}).

Then, we have the estimate

\|I_{45}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k,l}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

The combination of estimates from terms
I_{42}
to
I_{45}

and the estimate (
3.13
), it holds for all
k\geq 2
and
l\geq 1

\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k^{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

which yields the estimate (
3.17
).

Step 6:
Using the equation (
3.2
), then we have

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+2\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+2\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+2\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+2\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle I_{51}+I_{52}+I_{53}+I_{54}+I_{55}+I_{56}.

Using the a priori assumption (
3.11
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle I_{52}\leq C(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})(\|\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}};

\displaystyle I_{53}\leq k^{*}(\delta+\epsilon)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}((\|\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}});

\displaystyle I_{54}\leq\sqrt{L}\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}};

\displaystyle I_{55}\leq\sqrt{L}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}};

\displaystyle I_{56}\leq(k^{*})^{\frac{3}{2}}(\delta+\epsilon)^{\frac{3}{2}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}y}.

Combining estimates from
I_{52}
to
I_{56}
and using the estimates
(
A.4
) and (
3.16
), we can choose
\delta=\sqrt{L}
to obtain

\|\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

which yields the estimate (
3.18
).
Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Next, we will establish the energy estimate for the
approximated equation (
2.1
).

Lemma 3.3
.

For any smooth solution
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
of equations (
2.1
)-(
2.3
),
it holds true for all
x\in[0,L^{\epsilon}]

\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}\leq\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{16}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{16}),

(3.28)

where
k\geq 2
and
l\geq 1
.

Proof.

Taking
\partial_{x}^{k}
differential operator to the equation (
3.2
), we have

\partial_{x}^{k+1}\{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\}-\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}+\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}=0.

Multiplying the above equation by
\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}
and
integrating over
[0,x]\times[0,\infty)
, we have

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}((u^{\epsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2})(x,y)\langle y\rangle^{2l}dy-\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy

(3.29)

\displaystyle+\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\epsilon}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy

\displaystyle=\frac{1}{2}\int_{0}^{\infty}((u^{\epsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2})(0,y)\langle y\rangle^{2l}dy+II_{1}+II_{2}+II_{3}+II_{4},

where the terms
II_{i}(i=1,2,3,4)
are defined by

\displaystyle II_{1}:=\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy,

\displaystyle II_{2}:=-\sum_{j=1}^{k+1}C^{k+1}_{j}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j}\{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}\}\partial_{x}^{k+1-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy,

\displaystyle II_{3}:=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy,

\displaystyle II_{4}:=-\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}(\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}q^{\epsilon})\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy.

Next, we deal with the dissipative term.
For any
\epsilon>0
, we use the boundary condition (
2.2
) to obtain

\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|_{y=0}=\left.\partial_{x}^{k}\left\{\frac{\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}u^{\epsilon}-v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}\right\}\right|_{y=0}=0.

(3.30)

Then, we integrate by part to get

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy

(3.31)

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}|_{y=0}d\tau+\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{y}\{\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}\}d\tau dy

\displaystyle=

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}\{\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}+2\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\}\cdot\partial_{y}\{\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}\}d\tau dy.

Then, substituting the equality (
3.31
) into (
3.29
), we have

\displaystyle\underset{0\leq x\leq L}{\sup}\int_{0}^{\infty}(u^{\epsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dy+2\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\epsilon}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy+2\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}u^{\epsilon}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}|\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy

(3.32)

\displaystyle\leq\int_{0}^{\infty}((u^{\epsilon})^{2}|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2})(0,y)\langle y\rangle^{2l}dy+2\sum_{i=1}^{i=8}|II_{i}|,

where the terms
II_{i}(i=5,6,7,8)
are defined by

\displaystyle II_{5}:=-\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}(\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}q^{\epsilon})\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy,

\displaystyle II_{6}:=-2\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}(\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon})\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy,

\displaystyle II_{7}:=-\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy,

\displaystyle II_{8}:=-2l\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{k}\{\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}q^{\epsilon}+2\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}q\}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l-1}dxdy.

Now, we claim that the terms
II_{i}(i=1,...,8)
can be estimated as follows:

\displaystyle|II_{1}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

(3.33)

\displaystyle|II_{2}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{16}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{16}),

(3.34)

\displaystyle|II_{3}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

(3.35)

\displaystyle|II_{4}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

(3.36)

\displaystyle|II_{5}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

(3.37)

\displaystyle|II_{6}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

(3.38)

\displaystyle|II_{7}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

(3.39)

\displaystyle|II_{8}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

(3.40)

Then, substituting the estimates (
3.33
)-(
3.40
) into (
3.32
),
it is easy to obtain the estimate (
3.28
).
∎

In order to complete the proof of Lemma
3.3
, it remains to
show the claim estimates (
3.33
)-(
3.40
) as follows.

Proof of estimate (
3.33
)
.

It is easy to deduce that

|II_{1}|\leq\|\frac{\partial_{x}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2},

which, together with the estimates (
A.3
),
(
3.14
), (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), yields directly

|II_{1}|\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{8}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{8}),

which is the inequality (
3.33
).
∎

Proof of estimate (
3.34
)
.

Let us write

\displaystyle II_{2}=

\displaystyle-\sum_{j=1}^{[\frac{k+1}{2}]}\sum_{j_{1}=0}^{j}C^{k+1}_{j}C^{j}_{j_{1}}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j_{1}}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j-j_{1}}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k+1-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy

\displaystyle-\sum_{[\frac{k+1}{2}]}^{k+1}\sum_{j_{1}=0}^{j}C^{k+1}_{j}C^{j}_{j_{1}}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j_{1}}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{j-j_{1}}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k+1-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dxdy

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{21}+II_{22}.

Applying the estimates (
A.3
), (
3.14
), (
3.15
) and (
3.16
), we have

\displaystyle|II_{21}|\leq

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}^{2}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}}^{2})\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{16}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{16}).

Using Sobolev inequality, divergence-free condition,
estimates (
A.3
), (
A.4
) and (
3.13
), we have

\displaystyle|II_{22}|\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k+1\rangle}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k+1\rangle}u^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{1}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k+1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{16}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{16}).

Therefore, we can obtain the following estimate

|II_{2}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{16}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{16}),

which is inequality (
3.34
).
∎

Proof of estimate (
3.35
)
.

Apply the divergence-free condition, we have

II_{3}=\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy.

Due to
k\geq 2
, using the Sobolev inequality, estimates
(
A.4
), (
A.5
), (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), we have

\displaystyle|II_{3}|=

\displaystyle|\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy|

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k,k_{*}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}

\displaystyle+o_{L}(x)C_{k,k_{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\times(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\mathcal{E}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}_{l}(x))

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k^{*},k,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

This yields inequality (
3.35
) directly.
∎

Proof of estimate (
3.36
)
.

Using the divergence-free condition, we can write

\displaystyle II_{4}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{x}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}d\tau dy

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{41}+II_{42}.

Using Hölder inequality, divergence-free condition,
estimates (
A.4
) and (
A.6
), it holds true

\displaystyle|II_{41}|

\displaystyle\leq\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.41)

\displaystyle\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l}(\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}_{0}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}.

Due to
k\geq 2
, using the Hölder inequality, estimates
(
A.4
), (
A.6
), (
A.7
), (
3.13
) and (
3.14
), we have

\displaystyle|II_{42}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(3.42)

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}

\displaystyle+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\mathcal{E}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]+1\rangle}_{l}(x))\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},k,l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

Then, the combination of estimates (
3.41
)
and (
3.42
) yields the inequality (
3.36
).
∎

Proof of estimate (
3.37
)
.

Using the divergence-free condition, it holds true

\displaystyle II_{5}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{yy}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dydx

\displaystyle+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{2l}dydx

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{51}+II_{52}.

First of all, let us deal with the term
II_{51}
.
We can write

\displaystyle II_{51}=

\displaystyle-\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{yy}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi\langle y\rangle^{2l}dydx-\int_{0}^{L}\int_{0}^{\infty}\partial_{yy}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\cdot\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}(1-\chi)\langle y\rangle^{2l}dydx

\displaystyle:=

\displaystyle II_{511}+II_{512}.

Using the Hölder inequality, it holds true

|II_{511}|\leq C\|\frac{y}{\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\frac{1}{y}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

(3.43)

Using the Hardy inequality and estimate (
A.4
), it is easy to check that

\|\frac{1}{y}\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

(3.44)

On the other hand, using divergence-free condition,
relations (
3.11
) and (
3.12
), it holds true

\displaystyle\|\frac{y}{\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*}}\|\sqrt{y}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

(3.45)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k_{*}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}_{0}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}u^{\epsilon}_{0}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{y}})+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

Then, we can get that

|II_{511}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

(3.46)

Using Hölder inequality and estimate (
A.6
), it holds true

\displaystyle|II_{512}|\leq

\displaystyle C_{k^{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(x)\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}),

which, together with estimate (
3.46
), yields directly

|II_{51}|\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{4}+X^{k}_{l}(x)^{4}).

(3.47)
