\displaystyle C\int_{\psi}^{\infty}\Big|\frac{\phi_{\psi^{\prime}}(x,\psi^{\prime})}{\psi^{\prime\frac{1}{4}}}\chi(\frac{\psi^{\prime}}{\eta_{0}})\big(\frac{\phi_{\psi^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}}(x,\psi^{\prime})}{\psi^{\prime\frac{1}{4}}}\chi(\frac{\psi^{\prime}}{\eta_{0}})+\frac{\phi_{\psi^{\prime}}(x,\psi^{\prime})}{\psi^{\prime\frac{5}{4}}}\chi(\frac{\psi^{\prime}}{\eta_{0}})+\frac{\phi_{\psi^{\prime}}(x,\psi^{\prime})}{\psi^{\prime\frac{1}{4}}}\chi^{\prime}(\frac{\psi^{\prime}}{\eta_{0}})\big)\Big|d\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big).

Substituting (
4.48
) and (
4.55
) into (
4.53
), we have

\displaystyle\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

(4.56)

We then estimate
\|\sqrt{u}\omega_{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}
. Owing to

2u\omega_{\psi}=\phi_{\psi}-2\frac{\bar{u}_{y}}{\bar{u}}\omega,

we then conclude from (
4.16
), (
4.20
), (
4.55
) and (
4.56
) that

\displaystyle\|\sqrt{u}\omega_{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\bar{u}_{y}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\omega}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\big)

(4.57)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}(x,\psi)}{\sqrt{u(x,\psi)}}(1-\chi(\frac{\psi}{\eta_{0}}))\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}(x,\psi)}{\sqrt{u(x,\psi)}}\chi(\frac{\psi}{\eta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}(x,\psi)}{\psi^{\frac{1}{4}}}\chi(\frac{\psi}{\eta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\big)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

Invoking Sobolev inequality and (
4.57
), we obtain

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\big|\omega_{\psi}\phi_{x}\phi_{x\psi}\big|d\psi\leq

\displaystyle C\|\sqrt{u}\omega_{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}.

This, combining (
4.50
)-(
4.52
), leads to the estimate (
4.49
) by using equivalent relation
u\sim\bar{u}
and recalling the definition of
\mathcal{E}(x)
and
\mathcal{D}(x)
.
Thus, we have completed the proof of this lemma.
∎

Next, we can also obtain the following quotient estimate in
H^{1}
level.

Lemma 4.10
.

Under the assumption (
4.4
), we have

\sup_{x^{\prime}\in I}\|\frac{\phi_{x^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\int_{I}\big(\|\phi_{x^{\prime}\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x^{\prime}}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)dx^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{E}^{2}(0)+C\mathcal{E}(x)\mathcal{D}^{2}(x),

(4.58)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
x
.

Proof.

Multiplying (
4.43
) by
\frac{\phi_{x}}{u}
and integrating with respect to
\psi
, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dx}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u\bar{u}(u+\bar{u})}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\big|\omega_{x}\phi_{x}\phi_{\psi\psi}\big|}{u}+\frac{\big|\phi\phi_{x}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}+\frac{\big|\phi_{x}^{2}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{2}}\Big)d\psi.

Utilizing (
4.44
) into (
4.42
), we then calculate that

\displaystyle\|\omega_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

(4.59)

Hence, we get from (
4.31
) and (
4.59
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\omega_{x}\phi_{x}\phi_{\psi\psi}\big|}{u}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle\|\omega_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

Using (
4.59
) once again, together with (
4.13
) and (
4.20
), we compute

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\phi\phi_{x}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle\|\omega_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}.

Applying (
4.59
) once more, we discover that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\phi_{x}^{2}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{2}}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle\|\omega_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}.

Integrating and utilizing these estimates above, using the equivalent relation
u\sim\bar{u}
, then recalling the definition of
\mathcal{E}(x)
and
\mathcal{D}(x)
, we deduce (
4.58
) directly.
∎

Lemma 4.11
.

Under the assumption (
4.4
), we have

\sup_{x^{\prime}\in I}\big(\|\phi_{x^{\prime}\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x^{\prime}}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)+\int_{I}\|\frac{\phi_{x^{\prime}x^{\prime}}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}dx^{\prime}\leq\mathcal{E}^{2}(0)+C\mathcal{E}(x)\mathcal{D}^{2}(x),

(4.60)

where
C
is a positive constant independent of
x
.

Proof.

Multiplying (
4.43
) by
\frac{\phi_{xx}}{u}
and integrating with respect to
\psi
, we find that

\displaystyle\frac{1}{2}\frac{d}{dx}\big(\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u\bar{u}(u+\bar{u})}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)+\|\frac{\phi_{xx}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq C\int_{0}^{\infty}\Big(\frac{\big|\omega_{x}\phi_{xx}\phi_{\psi\psi}\big|}{u}+\frac{\big|\phi\phi_{xx}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}+\frac{\big|\phi_{x}^{2}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}\Big)d\psi.

(4.61)

Recalling now the equation
\eqref{eq3}_{1}
, the estimates (
4.37
) and (
4.38
) yields

\displaystyle\|u\phi_{\psi\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\leq C\big(\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\big)\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

(4.62)

We can calculate from (
4.47
) and (
4.62
) that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\omega_{x}\phi_{xx}\phi_{\psi\psi}\big|}{u}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle C\|\frac{\omega_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{xx}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|u\phi_{\psi\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{xx}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

Then, utilizing (
4.38
) with (
4.47
), we deduce

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\phi\phi_{xx}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle C\|\frac{\omega_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{xx}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi}{u^{2}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\|\frac{\phi_{xx}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\times\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big).

Making use of (
4.37
), (
4.46
) and (
4.47
), we discover

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{\infty}\frac{\big|\phi_{x}^{2}\omega_{x}\big|}{u^{4}}d\psi\leq

\displaystyle C\|\frac{\omega_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{xx}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{\frac{1}{2}}\|\phi_{x\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Pluging these estimates above into the estimate (
4.61
), recalling the fact that
u\sim\bar{u}
and the definition of
\mathcal{E}(x)
and
\mathcal{D}(x)
, it is easy to deduce the estimate (
4.60
).
∎

Under the a priori assumption (
4.4
), we collect all the estimates in
Lemmas
4.6
-
4.11
, to get that

\mathcal{D}(x)\leq\mathcal{E}(0)+C^{*}\mathcal{E}^{\frac{1}{2}}(x)\mathcal{D}(x),

which, together with the a priori assumption (
4.4
), leads to

\mathcal{D}(x)\leq\mathcal{E}(0)+C^{*}\sigma^{\frac{1}{2}}\mathcal{D}(x).

If we take
\sigma\leq\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4(C^{*})^{2}},\frac{1}{2^{\frac{21}{2}}{\gamma}^{\frac{17}{2}}\bar{C}},\frac{\gamma-1}{2^{\frac{19}{2}}{\gamma}^{\frac{19}{2}}\bar{C}}\Big\}
, then we have

\mathcal{E}(x)\leq\mathcal{D}(x)\leq 2\mathcal{E}(0).

(4.63)

Taking
\gamma:=2f(0)
and
\sigma:=4\mathcal{E}(0)
, one can apply the a priori estimate

f(x)\leq 4f(0),\quad\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 4\mathcal{E}(0)

(4.64)

to obtain the following estimate

f(x)\leq 2f(0),\quad\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 2\mathcal{E}(0).

(4.65)

Here we require the initial data is small enough to satisfy the condition

\mathcal{E}(0)\leq\sigma_{0}=\frac{1}{4}\min\Big\{\frac{1}{4(C^{*})^{2}},\frac{1}{2^{\frac{21}{2}}{\gamma}^{\frac{17}{2}}\bar{C}},\frac{\gamma-1}{2^{\frac{19}{2}}{\gamma}^{\frac{19}{2}}\bar{C}}\Big\}.

Therefore, we have established the closed estimate in this subsection.

4.2.  Global existence and exponential decay in von-Mises variable

In this section, our first aim is to establish the global existence of
\phi
. Next, we will use Lemmas
4.6
-
4.11
to show that the difference
u-\bar{u}
, as function of
(x,\psi)
, decays exponentially in
x
variable, which proves the decay estimates (
4.3
) in Proposition
4.2
.
This, together the proof of the global existence result, gives the proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Proof of Proposition
4.2
.

Suppose the assumptions in Proposition
4.2
hold on, it is easy to obtain the local existence of the solution
\phi
.
Next, we use standard continuity argument to show the global well-posedness.
From the local existence result in Proposition
4.1
and
initial conditions in Proposition
4.2
, it holds on

f(x)\leq 4f(0)~~\text{and}~~\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 4\mathcal{E}(0),~~\forall x\in[0,x_{1}).

Set

x^{*}:=\sup_{x_{1}}\big\{x_{1}|f(x)\leq 4f(0)~~\text{and}~~\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 4\mathcal{E}(0),~\forall x\in[0,x_{1})\big\}.

We claim that
x^{*}=+\infty
. Otherwise, applying the estimate (
4.65
) and
the local existence result in Proposition
4.1
,
there exists a positive constant
x^{**}
, such that
x^{**}>x^{*}
,
it holds that for any
x^{*}<x<x^{**}
,

f(x)\leq 4f(0)~~\text{and}~~\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 4\mathcal{E}(0),~~\forall x\in[0,x_{1}).

This contradicts the definition of
x^{*}
. Thus, we complete the proof of the global result in Proposition
4.2
. Then we conclude from the estimates of Lemmas
4.6
-
4.11
that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}\mathcal{E}(x)+\bar{\mathcal{D}}(x)\leq 0,

where
\bar{\mathcal{D}}(x):=\sum_{k=0,1}\Big(\|\sqrt{u}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{\psi}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{k}\phi}{u}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{\psi}\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{k}\phi}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{k+1}\phi}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\Big)
.
Since
u\lesssim 1
, by the definition of
\mathcal{E}(x)
and
\bar{\mathcal{D}}(x)
, we note that there exists a positive constant independent of
x
such that
\mathcal{E}(x)\leq C\bar{\mathcal{D}}(x)
. This, together with the above inequality directly, implies that

\displaystyle\frac{d}{dx}\mathcal{E}(x)+\mathcal{E}(x)\leq 0.

We then employ Gronwall inequality, which yields the decay estimate (
4.3
) immediately. Therefore, we finish the proof of Proposition
4.2
.
∎

4.3.  Proof of Theorem
1.2

With the help of Proposition
4.2
, we are ready to prove Theorem
1.2
.
By virtue of the strong solution
\phi
obtained in Proposition
4.2
, we have

u(x,\psi)=\big(\phi(x,\psi)+\bar{u}^{2}(\psi)\big)^{\frac{1}{2}}.

Then for a given value of
\psi
, let
y
denotes the physical variable for the flows
u
, that is

y=\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{d\psi^{\prime}}{u(x,\psi^{\prime})}.

Indeed, this transformation is one-to-one between the regious
(x,\psi)\in[0,+\infty)\times[0,+\infty)
and
(x,y)\in[0,+\infty)\times[0,+\infty)
.
Then for
0\leq x<+\infty
and
0\leq y<+\infty
, we have

u(x,y)=u(x,\psi(x,y)),~~v(x,y)=\int_{0}^{y}u_{x}(x,y^{\prime})dy^{\prime}.

(4.66)

And the pair
(u,v)
definies a global strong solution to the magnetic Prandtl model (
1.6
).
Furthermore, we can obtain the strong nonlinear asymptotic stability of classical Hartmann boundary layer in the physical variable, see Theorem
1.2
. This is coincide with pointwise perturbations
u(x,y)-\bar{u}(y)
and
v(x,y)
.

More specifically, we have already shown the exponential decay estimate in
x
variable of
u
and
\bar{u}
as functions of
(x,\psi)
in Section
4.2
.
In order to obtain that
u
and
\bar{u}
, as functions of
(x,y)
, decay in same rate in
x
variable, we need to investigate the relation between
y
and
\psi
.
For any given value of
\psi
, we set

y_{1}:=\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{1}{u(x,\psi^{\prime})}d\psi^{\prime},\ y_{2}:=\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{1}{\bar{u}(\psi^{\prime})}d\psi^{\prime},

(4.67)

where
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
represent the physical variables for the flows
u
and
\bar{u}
, respectively.
As a function of
(x,\psi)
or
\psi
, it is easy to check that
y_{1}(x,\psi)\not\equiv y_{2}(\psi)
.
Clearly, for any given
y_{1}
, we have

\displaystyle|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|\leq|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})|+|\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|.

(4.68)

Recalling the definition (
4.67
) of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
, then using the fact that
u\lesssim 1
, (
4.16
), (
4.20
) and the decay estimate (
4.3
), we deduce that

\displaystyle|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})|=|uu_{\psi}-\bar{u}\bar{u}_{\psi}|(x,\psi)\leq C\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{\infty}_{\psi}}\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\leq Ce^{-x}.

(4.69)

Next return to estimate the second term on the right handside of (
4.68
). Without loss of generality, we assume that
y_{1}<y_{2}
. Employing Mean value theorem of differential, we conclude that there exists
y^{*}\in[y_{1},y_{2}]
, such that

\displaystyle|\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|\leq C|\bar{u}_{yy}(y^{*})(y_{1}-y_{2})|\leq C|e^{-y^{*}}(y_{1}-y_{2})|.

(4.70)

We recall the definition (
4.67
) of
y_{1}
and
y_{2}
, by using the estimates (
4.13
), (
4.20
) and the decay estimate (
4.3
), to deduce that

\begin{split}|e^{-y^{*}}(y_{1}-y_{2})|\leq&\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{e^{-y_{1}}|u-\bar{u}|}{u\bar{u}}d\psi^{\prime}\leq C\Big(\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|\omega|^{2}}{u^{3}}d\psi^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\Big(\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{e^{-2y_{1}}}{u}d\psi^{\prime}\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\\
\leq&C\|\frac{\omega}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\Big(\int_{0}^{y_{1}}e^{-2y}dy\Big)^{\frac{1}{2}}\leq C\|\frac{\omega}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\leq C\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\\
\leq&C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\sqrt{u}\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}+\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}\big)\leq Ce^{-x}.\end{split}

(4.71)

Combining (
4.70
) and (
4.71
), we obtain that

|\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{1})|\leq Ce^{-x}.

(4.72)

It may be a wonder why we can obtain the decay estimate in
x
variable of
\bar{u}
.
Although
\bar{u}
is a function only depending on the space-like variable
y
,
y_{1}(x,\psi)
is a function depending on
x
, see the definition (
4.67
). Thus, we can get the exponential decay estimate (
4.72
) in
x
variable of
\bar{u}
.
Insert (
4.69
) and (
4.72
) into (
4.68
), we find

\|u_{y}(x,y)-\bar{u}_{y}(y)\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq Ce^{-x}.

It follows from the same way as (
4.68
) that for any
k=0,1,2
,

\|\partial_{y}^{k}(u-\bar{u})(x,y_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{y_{1}}}\leq\|\partial_{y}^{k}u(x,y_{1})-\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{2})\|_{L^{2}_{y_{1}}}+\|\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{2})-\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{y_{1}}}.

(4.73)

Invoking (
4.70
) and (
4.71
), it is easy to see that the second term on the right handside of (
4.73
) can be controlled as follows:

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}|\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{2})-\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{1})|^{2}dy_{1}\leq C\int_{0}^{y}|\partial_{y}^{k+1}\bar{u}(y^{*})(y_{1}-y_{2})|^{2}dy_{1}

(4.74)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{y}|e^{-y^{*}}(y_{1}-y_{2})|^{2}dy_{1}\leq C\int_{0}^{y}e^{-y_{1}}\Big|\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}y_{1}}|u-\bar{u}|}{u\bar{u}}d\psi^{\prime}\Big|^{2}dy_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\sup_{y}\Big|\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}y_{1}}|u-\bar{u}|}{u\bar{u}}d\psi^{\prime}\Big|^{2}\int_{0}^{y}e^{-y_{1}}dy_{1}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|\omega|^{2}}{u^{3}}d\psi^{\prime}\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{e^{-\frac{1}{2}y_{1}}}{u}d\psi^{\prime}\leq\|\frac{\omega}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\int_{0}^{y_{1}}e^{-\frac{1}{2}y}dy\leq C\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq Ce^{-2x}.

We now proceed to estimate the first term on the right handside of (
4.73
). We then deduce from the decay estimate (
4.3
), the estimates (
4.13
) and (
4.20
) that for
k=0
,

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}|u(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}(y_{2})|^{2}dy_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|u(x,\psi^{\prime})-\bar{u}(\psi^{\prime})|^{2}}{u}d\psi^{\prime}\leq\|\frac{\omega}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\omega}{u^{\frac{3}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq C\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq Ce^{-2x}.

For
k=1
, we employ the decay estimate (
4.3
), the estimates (
4.13
) and (
4.20
) once again, and combine with (
4.15
), to compute

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}|u_{y}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{y}(y_{2})|^{2}dy_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|uu_{\psi^{\prime}}(x,\psi^{\prime})-\bar{u}\bar{u}_{\psi^{\prime}}(\psi^{\prime})|^{2}}{u}d\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\|\frac{\phi_{\psi}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+C\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{x}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)\leq Ce^{-2x}.

For
k=2
, in view of the equation (
1.9
), the decay estimate (
4.3
), the estimates (
4.13
) and (
4.20
), we calculate that

\displaystyle\int_{0}^{y}|u_{yy}(x,y_{1})-\bar{u}_{yy}(y_{2})|^{2}dy_{1}\leq\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|u(u^{2})_{\psi^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}}-\bar{u}(\bar{u})^{2}_{\psi^{\prime}\psi^{\prime}}|^{2}(x,\psi^{\prime})}{u(x,\psi^{\prime})}d\psi^{\prime}=\int_{0}^{\psi}\frac{|\phi_{x}+2\omega|^{2}}{u}d\psi^{\prime}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C\big(\|\frac{\phi}{u^{\frac{5}{2}}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)\leq C\big(\|\phi\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\phi_{\psi}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}+\|\frac{\phi_{x}}{\sqrt{u}}\|_{L^{2}_{\psi}}^{2}\big)\leq Ce^{-2x}.

By collecting three estimates above, we can get that for
k=0,1,2
,

\|\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{2})-\partial_{y}^{k}\bar{u}(y_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{y_{1}}}\leq Ce^{-x}.

(4.75)

Substituting (
4.74
) and (
4.75
) into (
4.73
), we then obtain

\|\partial_{y}^{k}(u-\bar{u})(x,y_{1})\|_{L^{2}_{y_{1}}}\leq Ce^{-x},\quad k=0,1,2.

Therefore, we complete the proof of Theorem
1.2
.

Appendix A
Some usefull estimates

First, we will state without proof two elementary Hardy type inequalities, refer to
Lemma B.1 in
[
39
]
.

Lemma A.1
.

Let
f:\mathbb{R}^{+}\rightarrow\mathbb{R}
be some proper function. Then

(i)
If
\lambda>-\frac{1}{2}
and
\underset{y\rightarrow+\infty}{\lim}f(y)=0
, then

\|f(1+y)^{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{y}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq\frac{2}{2\lambda+1}\|f(1+y)^{\lambda+1}\partial_{y}f\|_{L^{2}_{y}(\mathbb{R}^{+})};

(A.1)

(ii)
If
\lambda<-\frac{1}{2}
, then

\|f(1+y)^{\lambda}f\|_{L^{2}_{y}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}\leq\sqrt{-\frac{1}{2\lambda+1}}|f|_{y=0}-\frac{1}{2\lambda+1}\|f(1+y)^{\lambda+1}\partial_{y}f\|_{L^{2}_{y}(\mathbb{R}^{+})}.

(A.2)

Second, we prove some estimates as follows.

Proposition A.2
.

Let
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
be the smooth solution, defined on
[0,L^{\epsilon}]
,
to the approximated equations (
3.2
), (
2.2
) and (
2.3
).
Under the assumption conditions (
3.11
) and (
3.12
),
then it holds for integers
s\geq 0
and
l\geq 1
that

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}};

(A.3)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}});

(A.4)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}});

(A.5)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}});

(A.6)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}});

(A.7)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k_{*},k^{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

(A.8)

Here we require
L
satisfying
L\leq\delta_{0}^{2}
in estimates
(
A.4
) and (
A.6
).

Proof.

Step 1:
For any integer
s\geq 0
, it holds for
any cut off function
\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})
(see definition in (
1.17
)):

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}[1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})]\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}.

(A.9)

Using the equivalent relation (
3.11
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{k_{*}(y+\epsilon)}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\frac{1}{k_{*}}\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{y}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}

(A.10)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k_{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}_{0})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\frac{C_{k_{*}}}{\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}\chi^{\prime}(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}},

and

\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}[1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})]\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\frac{1}{k_{*}(\delta_{0}+\epsilon)}\|{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}[1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})]\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\frac{C_{k_{*}}}{\delta_{0}}\|{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}[1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta_{0}})]\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}.

(A.11)

Substituting estimates (
A.10
) and (
A.11
) into (
A.9
),
and applying the Sobolev embedding inequality, it holds true

\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}})\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}),

which implies directly

\|\frac{\partial_{x}^{s+1}u^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}H^{2}_{y}}.

Therefore, we complete the proof of estimate (
A.3
).

Step 2: For any integer
s\geq 0
and
\delta\leq\delta_{0}
,
we can apply the estimate (
3.12
) to get that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}},

(A.12)

and

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\frac{1}{k_{*}(\delta+\epsilon)}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

(A.13)

Integrating by part, we can get that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}=\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})^{2}\partial_{y}\{y\}dy

\displaystyle=-\int_{0}^{\infty}2\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})^{2}ydy-\int_{0}^{\infty}2|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|^{2}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\chi^{\prime}(\frac{y}{\delta})\frac{y}{\delta}dy,

which, together with the estimate (
3.11
), yields directly

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\chi\|_{L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq C(\sqrt{\delta}\|\sqrt{y}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+{\delta}^{-1}\|y\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}})

(A.14)

\displaystyle\leq C_{k_{*}}({\delta}^{-1}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\sqrt{\delta}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}).

Substituting estimates (
A.14
) and (
A.13
) into (
A.12
), we have

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*}}(\delta^{-1}\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\sqrt{\delta}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}),

(A.15)

which, integrating over
[0,L]
, implies that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*}}(\delta^{-1}L\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\sqrt{\delta}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

Choosing
\delta=\sqrt{L}\leq\delta_{0}
in the above inequality,
then we have

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

On the other hand, it is easy to check that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}_{0}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)\|\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

This, together with the estimate (
A.15
) and
\delta=\delta_{0}
, yields directly

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}_{0}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}).

Thus, we complete the proof of claimed estimates (
A.4
) and (
A.5
).

Step 3: It is easy to check that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}^{2}\leq 2\int_{0}^{\infty}|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}||\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}|dy\leq 2\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

Using the Hardy inequality (
A.2
), it holds true

\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{-1}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

Thus, we get that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq C_{l}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

This together with the estimates (
A.4
) and (
A.5
) gives respectively

\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l,\delta_{0}}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s+1}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}),

and

\|\partial_{x}^{s}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\leq o_{L}(1)C_{k_{*},l}(\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}),

for all
L\leq\delta_{0}^{2}
.
Therefore, we complete the proof the claimed estimates (
A.6
) and (
A.7
).

Step 4: For any inteter
s\geq 0
, it holds

\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}=\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})+\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})).

Using the assumptions (
3.11
) and (
3.12
),
it is easy to check that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\frac{1}{\{k_{*}(\delta+\epsilon)\}^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\sqrt{u}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}},

and

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\chi(\frac{y}{\delta})\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k_{*},k^{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

Therefore, we can get that

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq\frac{C_{k_{*}}}{\delta^{\frac{1}{2}}}\|\sqrt{u}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}(1-\chi(\frac{y}{\delta}))\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k_{*},k^{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{y}},

which, integrating over
[0,L]
and choosing
\delta=\delta_{0}
, yields directly

\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq{C_{k_{*},\delta_{0}}}\|\sqrt{u^{\epsilon}}\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{2}q^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k_{*},k^{*}}\|\partial_{x}^{s}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

which implies the claimed estimate (
A.8
).
Therefore, we complete the proof of this lemma.
∎

Let us define

V^{k}_{l}(x):=\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

(A.16)

and

\widehat{V}^{k}_{l}(0):=\|\partial_{y}^{\langle 2k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\sum_{\beta\geq 1,2\alpha+\beta\leq 2(k-1)}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}.

(A.17)

Proposition A.3
.

For any smooth solution
(u^{\epsilon},v^{\epsilon})
of equation (
3.2
),
then the following estimates hold true

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k,l}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+V^{k}_{l}(x)^{2});

(A.18)

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k,l}\mathcal{P}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+\widehat{V}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+V^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}),

(A.19)

where any integers
\alpha
and
\beta
satisfying
0\leq 2\alpha+\beta\leq 2k
.

Proof.

Step 1: Applying the differential operator
\partial_{x}^{k-1}

to the equation (
3.2
), we have

\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}=\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}+\partial_{x}^{k}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon})-\partial_{x}^{k}(v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}):=J_{1}+J_{2}+J_{3}.

Using the divergence-free condition, it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|J_{2}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+\sum_{j=1}^{k}C^{k}_{j}\|\partial_{x}^{j-1}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-j}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2},

and

\displaystyle\|J_{3}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{k}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}+\sum_{j=0}^{k-1}C^{k}_{j}\|\partial_{x}^{j}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{k-1-j}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{l}(\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{k}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\langle[\frac{k-1}{2}]\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(1+\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})

\displaystyle+C_{k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}).

Therefore, we can get that for all
l\geq 1

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{k-1}\partial_{y}^{4}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(1+\|\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})

\displaystyle+C_{k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-2\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})

\displaystyle+C_{k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k\rangle}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\langle k-1\rangle}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}).

This implies the estimate (
A.18
).

Step 2: We will give the proof of estimate (
A.19
) by induction.
Indeed, the estimate (
A.18
) implies that (
A.19
) holds true for
the case of
\beta=0,1,2
.
Now we suppose the estimate (
A.19
) holds true for the case

\beta\leq 2n
, i.e.,

\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\beta+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k,l}\mathcal{P}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+V^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}+\widehat{V}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}),

(A.20)

for
2\leq 2n\leq 2(k-1)
.
This and the relation
2\alpha+2n\leq 2k
implies the integer
\alpha\in[0,k-1]
.
We need to verify that (
A.19
) holds on for case of
\beta\leq 2n+1

and
\beta\leq 2n+2
, i.e.

\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{\beta+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq C_{k,l}\mathcal{P}(1+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}+V^{k}_{l}(x)^{2}+\widehat{V}^{k}_{l}(0)^{2}),

(A.21)

Indeed, the estimate (
A.20
) implies that (
A.21
)
holds true for the case
\beta\leq 2n
.
we just need to prove the estimate (
A.21
) holds on
for the case
\beta=2n+1
and
\beta=2n+2
.
Applying differential operator
\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n}
to
the equation (
3.2
), then we have

\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+3}v^{\epsilon}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon})-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n}(v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}):=K_{1}+K_{2}+K_{3}.

Using the Hölder inequality and divergence-free condition, then we have

\displaystyle\|K_{2}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sum_{j=0}^{2n}\|\partial_{y}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n-j}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k}\sum_{i=1}^{\alpha}\sum_{j=0}^{2n}\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{j}u^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-i}\partial_{y}^{2n-j}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{y}^{2n}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}(\|\partial_{y}^{2n}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle+C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}},

and

\displaystyle\|K_{3}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\sum_{j=0}^{2n}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{j}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}^{2n-j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k}\sum_{i=0}^{\alpha-1}\sum_{j=0}^{2n}\|\partial_{x}^{i}\partial_{y}^{j}v^{\epsilon}\partial_{x}^{\alpha-i}\partial_{y}^{2n-j}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{y}^{2n+1}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}+C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n}v^{\epsilon}\|_{L^{\infty}_{x}L^{\infty}_{y}}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}(\|\partial_{y}^{2n+1}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})

\displaystyle+C_{k}(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}})\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}.

Then, we can get that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}

(A.22)

\displaystyle\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(1+\|\partial_{y}^{2n+1}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})+C_{k,l}\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\langle 2n+2\rangle}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}.

Applying differential operator
\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}
to
the equation (
3.2
), then we have

\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+4}v^{\epsilon}=\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+3}v^{\epsilon}+\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}(u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon})-\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{2n+1}(v^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}).

Then, similar to the estimates of terms
K_{2}
and
K_{3}
,
it is easy to check that

\displaystyle\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+4}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}\leq

\displaystyle C_{k,l}(1+\|\partial_{y}^{2n+2}u^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+2}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}^{2})

(A.23)

\displaystyle+C_{k,l}(\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha}\partial_{y}^{\langle 2n+2\rangle}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}+\|\partial_{x}^{\alpha-1}\partial_{y}^{2n+3}v^{\epsilon}\langle y\rangle^{l}\|_{L^{2}_{x}L^{2}_{y}}^{2}).

Thus, the estimates (
A.22
) and (
A.23
) imply estimate (
A.21
)
holds true for
\beta=2n+1
and
\beta=2n+2
.
Therefore, we complete the proof of lemma.
∎

Appendix B
Compatibility of initial data

In this section, we will introduce the definition of compatibility
of initial data at the corner
(0,0)
. This can explain the reason
that the initial data required in Theorem
1.1
.
First of all, by evaluating the equation (
1.6
) at
y=0
,
we obtain the boundary condition:

(-\partial_{y}^{2}u-1)|_{y=0}=0.

Thus, we require the initial data
u_{0}(y)
satisfies the compatibility condition

(-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}(y)-1)|_{y=0}=0.

(B.1)

Then, we use the equation (
1.6
) to deduce that

u\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})|_{x=0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
.
Taking
\partial_{y}
operator to the equation (
1.6
)
and applying the divergence-free condition, we have

u\partial_{x}\partial_{y}u+v\partial_{y}^{2}u-\partial_{y}^{3}u+\partial_{y}u=0.

(B.2)

Evaluating the above equation at
y=0
, then we have

(-\partial_{y}^{3}u+\partial_{y}u)|_{y=0}=0.

Thus, we require the initial data
u_{0}(y)
satisfies the compatibility condition

(-\partial_{y}^{3}u_{0}(y)+\partial_{y}u_{0}(y))|_{y=0}=0.

(B.3)

Furthermore, we also have that
\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})|_{x=0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+})
.
The above two compatibilities on initial data is devoted to
ensuring that the quantities
u\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})|_{x=0}
and
\partial_{y}(\frac{v}{u})|_{x=0}

is not singularity at the point
y=0
.

Secondly, using the equation (
1.6
), we have

v(x,y)=u(x,y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u(x,y^{\prime})+u(x,y^{\prime})-1}{[u(x,y^{\prime})]^{2}}dy^{\prime},

which implies

v_{0}(y):=v(0,y)=u_{0}(y)\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}(y^{\prime})+u_{0}(y^{\prime})-1}{[u_{0}(y^{\prime})]^{2}}dy^{\prime}.

(B.4)

By taking
\partial_{x}
differential operator to the equation
(
1.6
) and using the divergence-free condition, we obtain

\partial_{x}(u\partial_{x}u+v\partial_{y}u)+\partial_{y}^{3}v-\partial_{y}v=0,

(B.5)

which, evaluating at
y=0
, yields directly

(\partial_{y}^{3}v-\partial_{y}v)|_{y=0}=0.

The first order compatibility condition that arises from this
entails matching now the initial data evaluated at
y=0
with
boundary data evaluated at
x=0
in the standard manner for
initial boundary value problem:

\{\partial_{y}^{3}v_{0}(y)-\partial_{y}v_{0}(y)\}|_{y=0}=0.

(B.6)

On the other hand, using the divergence-free condition, we can
rewrite the equation as the form

u^{2}\partial_{xy}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)=2u\partial_{y}v\partial_{y}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)+\partial_{y}^{3}v-\partial_{y}v.

It is clear that all quantities on the righthand side of equation (
B.5
)
are vanishing at
y=0
.
Then, the first compatibility on initial data implies directly

u\partial_{xy}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)|_{x=0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

Taking
\partial_{x}
operator of equation (
B.2
) and using the
divergence-free condition, then we have

\partial_{x}(u\partial_{x}\partial_{y}u+v\partial_{y}^{2}u)+\partial_{y}^{4}v-\partial_{y}^{2}v=0.

which, evaluating at
y=0
, yields directly

(\partial_{y}^{4}v-\partial_{y}^{2}v)|_{y=0}=0.

This then gives our second order compatibility condition:

\{\partial_{y}^{4}v_{0}(y)-\partial_{y}^{2}v_{0}(y)\}|_{y=0}=0.

(B.7)

Taking
\partial_{y}
operator to the equation, we have

\partial_{y}\left\{u^{2}\partial_{xy}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)\right\}=\partial_{y}\left\{2u\partial_{y}v\partial_{y}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)\right\}+\partial_{y}^{4}v-\partial_{y}^{2}v.

(B.8)

It is clear that all quantities on the righthand side of equation (
B.8
)
are vanishing at
y=0
.
Then, the second compatibility on initial data implies directly

\partial_{xy}\left(\frac{v}{u}\right)|_{x=0}\in L^{2}(\mathbb{R}^{+}).

Therefore, we can define the higher order compatibility conditions at the
corner
(0,0)
in the same manner, and can be stated as follows

\left\{\partial_{y}^{3}(\partial_{x}^{m}v|_{x=0})-\partial_{y}(\partial_{x}^{m}v|_{x=0})\right\}|_{y=0}=0,

(B.9)

and

\left\{\partial_{y}^{4}(\partial_{x}^{m}v|_{x=0})-\partial_{y}^{2}(\partial_{x}^{m}v|_{x=0})\right\}|_{y=0}=0,

(B.10)

for all
m\geq 0
.
It is worth noticing that the higher order
x-
derivative of
v

at
x=0
can be controlled by the lower one.
The compatibility conditions (
B.9
) and (
B.10
)
on the initial data are called the
(2m+1)-
th and
(2m+2)-
th order
compatibility conditions at the corner
(0,0)
.
According to the initial norms
{\mathcal{B}}^{k}_{l}(0)

and
{\mathcal{E}}^{k}_{l}(0)
(see the definitions in (
3.5
)
and (
3.7
)), we require the generic compatibility
conditions at the corner
(0,0)
up to order
2k-1
in Theorem
1.1
.
Finally, we explain the initial data
u_{0}
satisfying the
compatibility condition. Obviously, we have

\partial_{y}v=\partial_{y}u\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u+u-1}{(u)^{2}}d\tau+\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u+u-1}{u},

and hence, it holds

\partial_{y}v_{0}|_{y=0}=\left\{\partial_{y}u_{0}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{(u_{0})^{2}}d\tau+\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}}\right\}_{y=0}=0,

where we have used the compatibility conditions (
B.1
) and (
B.3
).
This and the compatibility condition (
B.6
) implies

\partial_{y}^{3}v_{0}|_{y=0}=0
. Recall that

\partial_{y}^{3}v_{0}=\partial_{y}^{3}u_{0}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}^{2}}d\tau+\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}^{2}}-\partial_{y}u_{0}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{3}u_{0}+\partial_{y}u_{0}}{u_{0}^{2}}+\frac{-\partial_{y}^{4}u_{0}+\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}}{u_{0}},

then we require the initial data of
u_{0}
to satisfy

(-\partial_{y}^{4}u_{0}+\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0})|_{y=0}=(-\partial_{y}^{5}u_{0}+\partial_{y}^{3}u_{0})|_{y=0}=0.

Thus, this ensures
\partial_{y}^{3}v_{0}|_{y=0}=0
that will satisfy the compatibility condition.
Similarly, we require the initial data
u_{0}
satisfying

(-\partial_{y}^{6}u_{0}+\partial_{y}^{4}u_{0})|_{y=0}=(-\partial_{y}^{7}u_{0}+\partial_{y}^{5}u_{0})|_{y=0}=0,

to ensure the compatibility (
B.7
).
Similarly, we can add conditions to the initial data
u_{0}
to satisfy
the compatibility conditions (
B.9
) and (
B.10
).

Appendix C
Control of initial data

In this section, we will prove that the initial condition

\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)+\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(0)
can be controlled be the initial data

u_{0}
, and hence is independent of
\epsilon
.
That is to give the proof for the claim estimate as follows
(see (
3.56
))

\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(0)+\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)\leq C(u_{0}),

(C.1)

where the constant
C(u_{0})
only depends on the initial data
u_{0}
.

Proof.

First of all, it is easy to check that

|\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})|_{x=0}=|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}|_{x=0}=|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{(u_{0}+\epsilon)^{2}}|\leq|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}^{2}}|.

This, together with the compatibility conditions
(
B.1
) and (
B.3
), yields directly

\|\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})\langle y\rangle^{l}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C(u_{0}).

(C.2)

Secondly, according to the equation (
2.1
), we have

v^{\epsilon}=u^{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}d\tau,

which implies directly

\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}=\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}d\tau+\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon}{u^{\epsilon}}.

Then, it holds

\displaystyle|\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}

\displaystyle\leq|\partial_{y}u_{0}|\int_{0}^{y}|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{(u_{0}+\epsilon)^{2}}|d\tau+|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}+\epsilon}|

\displaystyle\leq|\partial_{y}u_{0}|\int_{0}^{y}|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{(u_{0})^{2}}|d\tau+|\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u_{0}+u_{0}-1}{u_{0}}|,

which, together with compatibility condition (
B.3
), yields directly

\|\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C(u_{0}).

(C.3)

Similarly, it is easy to deduce that

\|\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C(u_{0}).

(C.4)

It is easy to check that

\displaystyle\partial_{xy}v^{\epsilon}=

\displaystyle-\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}d\tau+\frac{(\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon})u^{\epsilon}+(-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-\epsilon-1)\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}}{(u^{\epsilon})^{2}}

\displaystyle+\partial_{y}u^{\epsilon}\int_{0}^{y}\frac{(\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon})(u^{\epsilon})^{2}+2u^{\epsilon}(-\partial_{y}^{2}u^{\epsilon}+u^{\epsilon}-1-\epsilon)\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}}{(u^{\epsilon})^{4}}d\tau.

This, together with the estimates (
C.2
)-(
C.4
)
and compatibility condition (
B.7
), yields directly

\|\partial_{xy}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C(u_{0}).

(C.5)

Similarly, it is easy to deduce that

\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{2}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}+\|\partial_{x}\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}_{y}}\leq C(u_{0}).

(C.6)

Finally, using the equation (
2.1
), it is easy to check that

\displaystyle|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{xy}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})|_{x=0}

\displaystyle\leq|\frac{2u^{\epsilon}\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}+\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}}|_{x=0}

\displaystyle\leq 2|\partial_{y}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})|_{x=0}|\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon}|_{x=0}+|\frac{(\partial_{y}^{3}v^{\epsilon}-\partial_{y}v^{\epsilon})}{u_{0}+\epsilon}|_{x=0},

This, together with the estimates (
C.2
)-(
C.4
)
and compatibility condition (
B.7
), yields directly

\|u^{\epsilon}\partial_{xy}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(u_{0}).

Similarly, we can also obtain

\|\partial_{xy}(\frac{v^{\epsilon}}{u^{\epsilon}})|_{x=0}\|_{L^{2}}\leq C(u_{0}).

The other terms in
\mathcal{E}^{k}_{l}(0)
and
\mathcal{B}^{k}_{l}(0)

can be controlled by the similar way, for the sake of
simplicity, we refrain from writing. Therefore, we complete the proof of
the claim estimate (
C.1
).
∎
